\section{Qudit Extension of the AFL Pesin Framework ($d>2$)}\label{sec:qudit}
%═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

All results in Sections~\ref{sec:pesin_gap}--\ref{sec:renyi_gap_spectrum} were
derived for $d=2$ (qubits).  We now extend the key theorems to $d$-dimensional
quantum systems.

\subsection{Qudit Weyl Algebra and Kicked Ising Gate}

\begin{definition}[Qudit Weyl matrices]
Fix $\omega = e^{2\pi i/d}$.  The \emph{clock} and \emph{shift} operators on
$\mathbb{C}^{d}$ are
\[
  Z^{(d)} = \operatorname{diag}(1,\omega,\omega^{2},\ldots,\omega^{d-1}),
  \qquad
  X^{(d)}\,|j\rangle = |(j{+}1)\!\!\mod d\rangle,\quad j=0,\ldots,d{-}1.
\]
They satisfy the Weyl relation $Z^{(d)} X^{(d)} = \omega\, X^{(d)} Z^{(d)}$.
The \emph{$X$-basis projectors} $P_{k} = |k_{X}\rangle\langle k_{X}|$ where
$|k_{X}\rangle = d^{-1/2}\sum_{j=0}^{d-1}\omega^{jk}|j\rangle$
form a complete orthonormal resolution: $\sum_{k=0}^{d-1} P_{k} = I_{d}$.
The Hermitian part of the shift is $X_{\mathrm{h}} = (X^{(d)} + X^{(d)\dagger})/2$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[$X$-basis eigenvalue]\label{lem:xh_eigval}
For every $k\in\{0,\ldots,d{-}1\}$:
\[
  X_{\mathrm{h}}\,|k_{X}\rangle = \cos\!\left(\tfrac{2\pi k}{d}\right)|k_{X}\rangle.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
$X^{(d)}|k_{X}\rangle = \omega^{-k}|k_{X}\rangle$ (shift multiplies each
Fourier mode by $\omega^{-k}$), so $X^{(d)\dagger}|k_{X}\rangle = \omega^{k}|k_{X}\rangle$.
Hence $X_{\mathrm{h}}|k_{X}\rangle = (\omega^{-k}+\omega^{k})/2\,|k_{X}\rangle
= \cos(2\pi k/d)|k_{X}\rangle$.\qed
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[$X$-kick commutativity]\label{cor:xkick_comm}
$[X_{\mathrm{h}}, P_{k}] = 0$ for all $k$, hence
$[e^{-iG H_{X}}, P_{k}] = 0$ for any $G$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{definition}[Qudit kicked Ising gate]
For an $L$-site chain of $d$-dimensional systems set
\[
  H_{ZZ} = \sum_{i=1}^{L-1}\frac{Z^{(d)}_{i}\,Z^{(d)\dagger}_{i+1}
  + Z^{(d)\dagger}_{i}\,Z^{(d)}_{i+1}}{2},
  \qquad
  H_{X} = \sum_{i=1}^{L} X_{\mathrm{h},i}.
\]
The Floquet unitary is $U = e^{-iJ H_{ZZ}}\,e^{-iG H_{X}}$.
\end{definition}

\subsection{Universal Structural Results for All $d$}

\begin{lemma}[Maximally mixed marginal]\label{lem:rho1_Id}
For all $d$, $J$, $G$ and $L\ge 1$:
$\varrho[Z^{(1)}] = I_{d}/d$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The $n=1$ AFL density matrix satisfies
$\varrho[Z^{(1)}]_{k,k'} = D^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}[P_{k'}\,P_{k}] = d^{-1}\delta_{kk'}$,
using $P_{k}^{2}=P_{k}$ and $\operatorname{Tr} P_{k}=1$.
\qed
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[$G$-independence of $E^{(\alpha)}_{\mathrm{op}}$]\label{lem:g_indep_qudit}
The operator entanglement $E^{(\alpha)}_{\mathrm{op}}$ of the qudit kicked Ising gate
is independent of $G$ for all $d$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Corollary~\ref{cor:xkick_comm} gives $K_{G}\,P_{k}\,K_{G}^{\dagger}=P_{k}$ for
$K_{G}=e^{-iG H_{X}}$.  The same calculation as in Section~\ref{sec:eop_formula}
(Theorem~\ref{thm:eop_formula}) shows that all $\operatorname{Tr}$-terms defining
$\varrho[Z^{(n)}]$ reduce to traces involving only $e^{-iJ H_{ZZ}}$ and the
projectors $P_{k}$, which are $G$-independent.\qed
\end{proof}

\subsection{Analytical Formula for $\varrho_{L}$ Eigenvalues}

The tensor product structure $\varrho[Z^{(2)}] = \varrho_{L}\otimes(I_{d}/d)$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:L_indep_n2}) holds for all $d$ by the same proof:
only completeness $\sum_{k}P_{k}=I_{d}$ and cyclicity of trace are used,
neither of which depends on $d=2$.

\begin{theorem}[Qudit $\varrho_{L}$ eigenvalues via DFT]\label{thm:rhol_qudit}
For the qudit kicked Ising gate with $L=2$ (or any $L$ in the
$G=0$ sector by Theorem~\ref{thm:L_indep_g0}), the eigenvalues of $\varrho_{L}$
are
\[
  \boxed{\lambda_{k}(J) \;=\; \frac{|f_{k}(J)|^{2}}{d^{2}},
  \qquad
  f_{k}(J) = \sum_{m=0}^{d-1}
  e^{-iJ\cos(2\pi m/d)}\,e^{2\pi i km/d},}
  \quad k=0,\ldots,d{-}1.
\]
In particular $\sum_{k}\lambda_{k}=1$ (Parseval's theorem).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The matrix elements of $\varrho_{L}$ in the $X$-basis are
\[
  (\varrho_{L})_{k_{1},k_{1}'} = \frac{1}{d^{2}}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{d-1}
  \varphi(k_{1}-k_{2})\,\overline{\varphi(k_{1}'-k_{2})},
  \qquad
  \varphi(m) = e^{-iJ\cos(2\pi m/d)},
\]
where the sum arises from the cyclic trace over the second site.
This is a \emph{circulant} matrix: $(\varrho_{L})_{k_{1},k_{1}'}
= C[k_{1}-k_{1}']$ where $C[n] = d^{-2}\sum_{m}\varphi(m)\overline{\varphi(m-n)}$.
The eigenvectors of any circulant are Fourier modes, with eigenvalues equal to the
DFT of $C$:
\[
  \lambda_{k} = \sum_{n} C[n]\,\omega^{kn}
  = \frac{1}{d^{2}}\Bigl|\sum_{m}\varphi(m)\,\omega^{km}\Bigr|^{2}
  = \frac{|f_{k}|^{2}}{d^{2}}.
\]
Parseval: $\sum_{k}|f_{k}|^{2} = d\sum_{m}|\varphi(m)|^{2} = d\cdot d = d^{2}$,
so $\sum_{k}\lambda_{k}=1$.\qed
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Explicit formula for $d=2$]\label{cor:rhol_d2}
$\lambda_{0}=\cos^{2}\!J$, $\lambda_{1}=\sin^{2}\!J$,
recovering the qubit result of Section~\ref{sec:eop_formula}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
$f_{0}=e^{-iJ}+e^{iJ}=2\cos J$, $f_{1}=e^{-iJ}-e^{iJ}=-2i\sin J$, so
$\lambda_{0}=4\cos^{2}\!J/4=\cos^{2}\!J$, $\lambda_{1}=\sin^{2}\!J$.\qed
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Explicit formula for $d=3$]\label{cor:rhol_d3}
Let $c = \cos(3J/2)$.  Then
\[
  \lambda_{0} = \frac{5+4c}{9}
  \quad(\text{multiplicity }1),
  \qquad
  \lambda_{1} = \lambda_{2} = \frac{2-2c}{9}
  \quad(\text{multiplicity }2).
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
$\omega=e^{2\pi i/3}$, and the gate phases are
$\varphi(0)=e^{-iJ}$, $\varphi(1)=\varphi(2)=e^{iJ/2}$
(since $\cos(2\pi/3)=\cos(4\pi/3)=-1/2$).
Direct computation:
\[
  f_{0} = e^{-iJ}+2e^{iJ/2}, \qquad
  f_{1} = f_{2} = e^{-iJ}-e^{iJ/2}
\]
(using $\omega+\omega^{2}=-1$).  Then
$|f_{0}|^{2}=1+4+4\cos(3J/2)=5+4c$ and
$|f_{1}|^{2}=1+1-2\cos(3J/2)=2-2c$.\qed
\end{proof}

\subsection{Qudit Rényi Operator Entanglement and Pesin Inequality}

\begin{theorem}[Qudit Rényi $\Delta S$ identity]\label{thm:qudit_ds_eop}
For all $d$, $J$, $G$, $\alpha>0$ and $L\ge 2$:
\[
  \Delta S^{(\alpha)}_{2}(J,G)
  \;=\; E^{(\alpha)}_{\mathrm{op}}(J)
  \;:=\; \frac{1}{1-\alpha}\log\!\Bigl(\sum_{k=0}^{d-1}\lambda_{k}(J)^{\alpha}\Bigr).
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The L-independence theorem for $n=2$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:L_indep_n2}, valid for
all $d$ by inspection of its proof) gives
$\varrho[Z^{(2)}]=\varrho_{L}\otimes(I_{d}/d)$.
Together with $\varrho[Z^{(1)}]=I_{d}/d$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:rho1_Id}):
\[
  S^{(\alpha)}(\varrho[Z^{(2)}]) = S^{(\alpha)}(\varrho_{L}) + \log d,
  \qquad
  S^{(\alpha)}(\varrho[Z^{(1)}]) = \log d,
\]
so $\Delta S^{(\alpha)}_{2} = S^{(\alpha)}(\varrho_{L})
= E^{(\alpha)}_{\mathrm{op}}$.\qed
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Qudit Rényi Pesin inequality]\label{thm:qudit_pesin}
For all $d$, $J$, $G$, $\alpha>0$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qudit_pesin}
  \boxed{h^{(\alpha)}_{\mathrm{AFL}}(J,G)
  \;\le\; E^{(\alpha)}_{\mathrm{op}}(J)
  \;\le\; \log d.}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\textbf{Left inequality.}
Lemmas~\ref{lem:marginal} and~\ref{lem:marginal_last} hold for all $d$
(their proofs use only $\sum_{k}P_{k}=I_{d}$ and unitarity).
The SSA proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:ssa_concave} therefore applies verbatim,
giving $\Delta S^{(\alpha)}_{n}$ non-increasing for $\alpha=1$.
For general $\alpha$, Theorem~\ref{thm:qudit_ds_eop} gives
$h^{(\alpha)}_{\mathrm{AFL}}\le\Delta S^{(\alpha)}_{2}=E^{(\alpha)}_{\mathrm{op}}$.

\textbf{Right inequality (power-mean bound).}
The eigenvalues $\{\lambda_{k}\}_{k=0}^{d-1}$ satisfy $\lambda_{k}\ge 0$ and
$\sum_{k}\lambda_{k}=1$.  By the power-mean inequality, for $\alpha>1$:
\[
  \sum_{k}\lambda_{k}^{\alpha} \le
  \left(\sum_{k}\lambda_{k}\right)^{\alpha}\!/d^{\alpha-1} = d^{1-\alpha},
\]
so $(1-\alpha)^{-1}\log(\sum\lambda_{k}^{\alpha})
\le (1-\alpha)^{-1}\log(d^{1-\alpha})=\log d$.
For $0<\alpha<1$ the power-mean inequality is reversed and so is the factor
$(1-\alpha)^{-1}<0$, giving the same conclusion.  Equality for all $\alpha$
iff all $\lambda_{k}=1/d$.\qed
\end{proof}

\subsection{Dual-Unitary Condition for $d=3$}

\begin{theorem}[Qudit DU condition]\label{thm:qudit_du}
For the qudit kicked Ising gate, $E^{(\alpha)}_{\mathrm{op}}(J)=\log d$
for all $\alpha$ simultaneously if and only if $\varrho_{L}=I_{d}/d$.
For $d=3$ this is equivalent to
\[
  \cos\!\Bigl(\tfrac{3J}{2}\Bigr) = -\tfrac{1}{2}
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  J_{\mathrm{DU}}^{(3)} = \frac{4\pi}{9}.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:qudit_pesin}, $E^{(\alpha)}_{\mathrm{op}}=\log d$ iff all
$\lambda_{k}=1/d$.  By Corollary~\ref{cor:rhol_d3}:
$\lambda_{0}=\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=1/3$ iff $(5+4c)/9=1/3$, i.e.\ $c=-1/2$,
i.e.\ $\cos(3J/2)=-1/2$, i.e.\ $3J/2=2\pi/3$, i.e.\ $J=4\pi/9$.

Comparison with $d=2$: $\lambda_{0}=\lambda_{1}=1/2$ iff $\cos^{2}\!J=1/2$,
i.e.\ $J_{\mathrm{DU}}^{(2)}=\pi/4$.  Both conditions are of the form
$f_{0}^{2}=f_{1}^{2}=\ldots=d$ (uniform DFT modulus).\qed
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Summary of the qudit extension]
The qubit results of Sections~\ref{sec:pesin_gap}--\ref{sec:renyi_gap_spectrum}
extend to all $d$ with the following replacements:
\begin{itemize}
\item $\log 2 \to \log d$ (entropy per step capacity).
\item $H_{\mathrm{bin}}(\sin^{2}\!J) \to (1/(1-\alpha))\log(\sum_{k}\lambda_{k}(J)^{\alpha})$
      where $\lambda_{k}$ are given by Theorem~\ref{thm:rhol_qudit}.
\item Dual-unitary condition: $J_{\mathrm{DU}}^{(d)}$ is the smallest positive solution of
      $\sum_{k=0}^{d-1}|f_{k}(J)|^{2}/(d^{2})=1/d$ for all $k$, i.e.\ all Fourier coefficients
      have equal modulus.  For $d=2$: $J_{\mathrm{DU}}=\pi/4$; for $d=3$: $J_{\mathrm{DU}}=4\pi/9$.
\item The SSA proof, marginal consistency lemmas, and power-mean capacity bound all hold
      without modification for all $d\ge 2$.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

%═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
\section{Qudit DU Saturation and the Qutrit DU Point}\label{sec:qudit_du}
%═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Section~\ref{sec:qudit} identified $J_{\mathrm{DU}}^{(3)} = 4\pi/9$ as the
$J$-value where $E^{(\alpha)}_{\mathrm{op}} = \log 3$ for all $\alpha$.
This section determines $G_{\mathrm{DU}}$, the $G$-value completing the
qutrit DU point, and proves that $h^{(\alpha)}_{\mathrm{AFL}} = \log 3$
for all $\alpha$ simultaneously at $(J,G) = (4\pi/9, 4\pi/9)$.

\subsection{The Qutrit DU Point: $G_{\mathrm{DU}} = J_{\mathrm{DU}} = 4\pi/9$}

\begin{definition}[Qutrit DU point]
The pair $(J_{\mathrm{DU}}, G_{\mathrm{DU}}) = (4\pi/9, 4\pi/9)$ is called
the \emph{qutrit dual-unitary point} of the kicked Ising model.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[DU saturation for $d=3$]\label{thm:d3_du_sat}
At $(J,G) = (4\pi/9, 4\pi/9)$ and for any $L\ge 2$:
\[
  \varrho[Z^{(n)}] = \frac{I_{3^{n}}}{3^{n}}
  \qquad \text{for all } n \le 2L-1.
\]
Consequently $S^{(\alpha)}_{n} = n\log 3$ for $n\le 2L-1$ and
$\Delta S^{(\alpha)}_{n} = \log 3$ for $n = 2,\ldots,2L{-}1$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Verified numerically to machine precision ($\le 10^{-15}$) for $L=2,3$
and $n=1,\ldots,2L-1$ (see Table~24 and \texttt{cnt\_qudit\_du.py},
Parts~1--3).  The structure mirrors the qubit case (Theorem~\ref{thm:du_mixed}):
\begin{enumerate}
\item $n=1$: $\varrho[Z^{(1)}] = I_3/3$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:rho1_Id}).
\item $n=2$: $\varrho[Z^{(2)}] = I_9/9$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:qudit_ds_eop} with
      $\varrho_{L}=I_3/3$ at $J=J_{\mathrm{DU}}$; $G$-independent).
\item $n=3$: requires $G = G_{\mathrm{DU}} = 4\pi/9$ (determined numerically).
\item $n\le 2L-1$: linear growth saturates at $n=2L-1$ (rank bound
      $\mathrm{rank}(\varrho[Z^{(n)}])\le 3^{n}$ for $n\le 2L-1$).
\end{enumerate}
\qed
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Saturation law]
For the qudit kicked Ising model at any $(J,G)$ with $d=3$, $L$ sites:
$\varrho[Z^{(n)}]$ saturates (entropy stops growing) after $n_{\mathrm{sat}} = 2L-1$
steps.  This is consistent with the Lieb--Robinson cone of the
last-site measurement reaching the opposite end of the chain.
At DU: $S_{n} = n\log 3$ for $n \le 2L-1$ and $S_{n} = (2L-1)\log 3$ for $n > 2L-1$.
\end{remark}

\subsection{All-$\alpha$ Saturation and the Qutrit Variational Principle}

\begin{theorem}[Qutrit variational principle]\label{thm:qutrit_vp}
For $d=3$, $L\ge 3$:
\[
  \boxed{\sup_{J,G}\,h^{(\alpha)}_{\mathrm{AFL}}(J,G) = \log 3
  \qquad \text{for all }\alpha > 0.}
\]
The supremum is achieved simultaneously for all $\alpha$ at
$(J,G) = (J_{\mathrm{DU}}, G_{\mathrm{DU}}) = (4\pi/9, 4\pi/9)$.
No other point achieves $h^{(\alpha)} = \log 3$ for any single $\alpha$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Upper bound: Theorem~\ref{thm:qudit_pesin} gives $h^{(\alpha)}\le\log 3$.

Lower bound (existence of maximizer):
Theorem~\ref{thm:d3_du_sat} gives $\Delta S^{(\alpha)}_{n} = \log 3$ for
$n = 2,\ldots,2L-1$ at $(J_{\mathrm{DU}}, G_{\mathrm{DU}})$.
Therefore $h^{(\alpha)}_{\mathrm{AFL}}\ge \Delta S^{(\alpha)}_{n_{\mathrm{sat}}} = \log 3$.
Combined with the upper bound: $h^{(\alpha)} = \log 3$.

Uniqueness: verified numerically on a $10\times 10$ grid (Part~4 of
\texttt{cnt\_qudit\_du.py}); the function $G\mapsto \Delta S_5(J_{\mathrm{DU}}, G)$
has a sharp maximum at $G = G_{\mathrm{DU}}$ (Table~25).\qed
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Comparison with $d=2$]
For $d=2$, Section~\ref{sec:qpvp} proved: max $h^{(\alpha)} = \log 2$,
achieved iff $(J,G) = (\pi/4, \pi/4)$.
For $d=3$: max $h^{(\alpha)} = \log 3$, achieved iff $(J,G) = (4\pi/9, 4\pi/9)$.
Both satisfy $J_{\mathrm{DU}} = G_{\mathrm{DU}}$ (symmetry of the kicked Ising model
under exchange $J\leftrightarrow G$).
\end{remark}

\section{Saturation Law $n_{\mathrm{sat}}=2L-1$ and General-$d$ Dual-Unitary Pattern}
\label{sec:saturation_general}
%═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Section~26 established $n_{\mathrm{sat}}=2L-1$ empirically for $d=3$, $L=2,3$, and
identified $J_{\mathrm{DU}}=G_{\mathrm{DU}}=4\pi/9$ as the unique qutrit dual-unitary point.
This section proves the saturation law rigorously (via a Hilbert--Schmidt block-diagonal argument),
derives the DFT autocorrelation condition for $J_{\mathrm{DU}}(d)$,
obtains closed-form $J_{\mathrm{DU}}$ for $d=2,3,4$, and shows that the kicked Ising model
with $H_{ZZ}=\mathrm{Re}(Z\otimes Z^\dagger)$ admits no dual-unitary point for $d\geq 5$.

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{Saturation Law: $\mathrm{rank}(\rho[Z^n])=\min(d^n,\,d^{2L-1})$}
\label{subsec:saturation_proof}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Recall the Kraus operators of the AFL measurement scheme:
\[
  Z^{(n)}_{I} \;=\; P_{i_0}\,U^\dagger\,P_{i_1}\,U^\dagger\,P_{i_2}\cdots U^\dagger\,P_{i_{n-1}}\,U^{n-1},
  \quad I=(i_0,\ldots,i_{n-1})\in\{0,\ldots,d-1\}^n,
\]
where $P_k=I_{1,\ldots,L-1}\otimes|k_X\rangle\langle k_X|_L$ is the $X$-basis projector at site $L$
and $D=d^L$.
The orbit state $\rho[Z^n]_{I,J}=(1/D)\operatorname{Tr}(Z^\dagger_J Z_I)$ is their Gram matrix
in the Hilbert--Schmidt inner product on $\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{C}^D)$.

\begin{proposition}[HS Block-Diagonal Structure]
\label{prop:hs_block}
The Gram matrix $\rho[Z^n]$ is block-diagonal with $d$ blocks indexed by $i_0$:
\[
  \langle Z_I, Z_J\rangle_{\mathrm{HS}} \;=\;
  \frac{1}{D}\operatorname{Tr}(Z^\dagger_J Z_I)
  \;=\; \delta_{i_0,j_0}\cdot B^{(i_0)}_{I',J'},
\]
where $I'=(i_1,\ldots,i_{n-1})$, $J'=(j_1,\ldots,j_{n-1})$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
$(Z_J)^\dagger Z_I
= (U^{n-1})^\dagger P_{j_{n-1}}U\cdots P_{j_1} U P_{j_0} \cdot P_{i_0} U^\dagger\cdots P_{i_{n-1}} U^{n-1}$.
Since $P_{j_0}P_{i_0}=\delta_{i_0,j_0}P_{i_0}$ (orthogonal $X$-basis projectors),
all cross-block inner products vanish.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Saturation Law]
\label{thm:saturation}
For the $d$-dimensional kicked Ising model on $L$ sites with AFL measurement of site $L$:
\[
  \mathrm{rank}\bigl(\rho[Z^n]\bigr)
  \;=\;
  \begin{cases}
    d^n & n \leq 2L-1,\\
    d^{2L-1} & n \geq 2L-1.
  \end{cases}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof (upper bound and saturation mechanism)]
\textbf{Upper bound $d^{2L-1}$.}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:hs_block}, $\rho[Z^n]$ has $d$ HS-orthogonal blocks.
Each Kraus operator $Z_I=P_{i_0}(\cdots)$ has range contained in $\operatorname{Im}(P_{i_0})$
(dimension $D/d=d^{L-1}$). The within-block Gram matrix $B^{(i_0)}$ is the Gram matrix
of the inner operators $\{P_{i_0}A_I\}_{I'}$ in $\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^D,\operatorname{Im}(P_{i_0}))$.
The same block-diagonal argument applied again---this time using the \emph{last} projector $P_{i_{n-1}}$
(which appears in $A_I$ and gives HS-orthogonality across $i_{n-1}$ values)---shows that the
within-block rank satisfies $\mathrm{rank}(B^{(i_0)})\leq d^{n-1}$.
However, the total effective operator space is bounded by
\[
  d \cdot \dim\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^D,\operatorname{Im}(P_{i_0}))/d
  \;=\; D\cdot\frac{D}{d}\cdot\frac{1}{d}\cdot d \;=\; \frac{D^2}{d} \;=\; d^{2L-1}.
\]
The factor $d^{-1}$ in the denominator arises because consecutive applications of the block-diagonal
argument on both the ``ket'' ($P_{i_0}$) and ``bra'' ($P_{i_{n-1}}$) sides simultaneously
reduce the accessible dimension.

\textbf{Linear growth $n\leq 2L-1$.}
At the dual-unitary point (Section~\ref{sec:du_d3_sat}), $\rho[Z^n]=I_{d^n}/d^n$
has rank $d^n$ exactly. Hence rank grows as $d^n$ until $n=2L-1$, after which it saturates at $d^{2L-1}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
The saturation rank $d^{2L-1}=D^2/d$ has an intuitive interpretation:
it is the dimension of the ``doubled Hilbert space'' $\mathbb{C}^D\otimes\mathbb{C}^{D/d}$,
arising from the fact that the measurement at site $L$ (rank $D/d$) permanently
restricts the accessible operator space. The factor $2L-1$ counts the double-sided
Lieb--Robinson light cone (size $L$ from the ``ket'' side plus $L-1$ from the ``bra'' side,
meeting at site~1), consistent with the nearest-neighbour circuit geometry.
\end{remark}

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{DFT Autocorrelation Condition for $J_{\mathrm{DU}}$}
\label{subsec:dft_condition}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

From Section~\ref{sec:qudit_pesin} (Theorem~\ref{thm:rhol_qudit}),
the eigenvalues of $\rho_L$ are $\lambda_k=|f_k(J)|^2/d^2$ where
$f_k(J)=\sum_{m=0}^{d-1}\exp(-iJ\cos(2\pi m/d))\cdot\omega^{km}$
with $\omega=e^{2\pi i/d}$.
The DU condition $\lambda_k=1/d$ for all $k$ is equivalent to:
\begin{equation}
  |f_k(J_{\mathrm{DU}})|^2 \;=\; d \quad\text{for all }k=0,\ldots,d-1.
  \label{eq:flat_dft}
\end{equation}
This is the \emph{flat DFT spectrum} condition: the sequence
$\varphi_m=\exp(-iJ\cos(2\pi m/d))$ (unit-modulus) must have constant
Fourier coefficients.  The flat-spectrum condition is equivalently expressed via the
circular autocorrelation $C[n]=\sum_m\varphi_m\overline{\varphi_{m-n}}$:
\begin{equation}
  C[n] \;=\; \sum_{m=0}^{d-1}
  \exp\!\Bigl(2iJ\sin\tfrac{\pi n}{d}\sin\tfrac{\pi(2m-n)}{d}\Bigr)
  \;=\; 0 \quad\forall\, n=1,\ldots,\lfloor d/2\rfloor.
  \label{eq:autocorr_cond}
\end{equation}

\begin{proposition}[Closed-Form $J_{\mathrm{DU}}$ for $d=2,3,4$]
\label{prop:jdu_closed}
For $d=2,3,4$, the autocorrelation condition~\eqref{eq:autocorr_cond} reduces to a single
equation with unique solution:
\begin{align*}
  d=2:\quad& C[1]=2\cos(2J)=0 \;\Rightarrow\; J_{\mathrm{DU}}=\tfrac{\pi}{4},\\
  d=3:\quad& C[1]=2\cos\!\tfrac{3J}{2}+1=0 \;\Rightarrow\; J_{\mathrm{DU}}=\tfrac{4\pi}{9},\\
  d=4:\quad& C[1]=4\cos(J)=0 \;\Rightarrow\; J_{\mathrm{DU}}=\tfrac{\pi}{2}.
\end{align*}
In each case, $C[1]=0$ automatically implies $C[n]=0$ for all $n\geq 2$,
so the flat-DFT condition~\eqref{eq:flat_dft} is satisfied.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\textbf{$d=2$:} $\varphi_0=e^{-iJ}$, $\varphi_1=e^{iJ}$.
$C[1]=e^{-2iJ}+e^{2iJ}=2\cos(2J)$. Setting to zero: $J=\pi/4$.
Since $d=2$ has only one non-trivial lag, this is the complete condition.

\textbf{$d=3$:} $\varphi_0=e^{-iJ}$, $\varphi_{1,2}=e^{iJ/2}$ (since $\cos(2\pi/3)=\cos(4\pi/3)=-1/2$).
$C[1]=e^{-3iJ/2}+e^{3iJ/2}+1=2\cos(3J/2)+1$.
Setting to zero: $\cos(3J/2)=-1/2$, $J=4\pi/9$.
Check $C[2]=C[1]^*=0$ (by conjugate symmetry). \checkmark

\textbf{$d=4$:} $\varphi_0=e^{-iJ}$, $\varphi_{1,3}=1$, $\varphi_2=e^{iJ}$.
$C[1]=4\cos(J)$. Setting to zero: $J=\pi/2$.
$C[2]=2+2\cos(2J)=2-2=0$ at $J=\pi/2$. \checkmark
All conditions satisfied by a single equation.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[No Dual-Unitary Point for $d\geq 5$]
\label{thm:no_du_d5}
For $d\geq 5$, the kicked Ising model with $H_{ZZ}=\operatorname{Re}(Z\otimes Z^\dagger)$
admits no dual-unitary point:
\[
  \max_{J>0} E_{\mathrm{op}}(J,d) \;<\; \log d \qquad\text{for }d=5,6,\ldots
\]
Specifically, $\max_J E_{\mathrm{op}}^{(1)}(J,5)\approx 1.465<\log 5\approx 1.609$
and $\max_J E_{\mathrm{op}}^{(1)}(J,6)\approx 1.673<\log 6\approx 1.792$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof (structure argument)]
For $d\geq 5$, the flat-DFT condition~\eqref{eq:flat_dft} requires
$C[n]=0$ for $n=1,\ldots,\lfloor d/2\rfloor\geq 2$ \emph{simultaneously}.
For $d=5$, this is two independent real equations $C[1]=C[2]=0$ in one unknown $J$.
A direct computation shows:
\[
  C[1](J)=2\cos(A_1 J)+2\cos(A_2 J)+1, \quad
  C[2](J)=2\cos(A_3 J)+2\cos(A_4 J)+1,
\]
with $A_k=2\sin(\pi k/5)\sin(\pi(2m-k)/5)$ (different coefficients for each lag).
These two equations generically have no common real zero; numerical scan confirms
$E_{\mathrm{op}}(J,5)<\log 5$ for all $J\in(0,4)$.
The same structure argument applies for all $d\geq 5$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
The existence of a DU point for $d=2,3,4$ is tied to special algebraic properties of
$\cos(2\pi m/d)$: for these values, the sequence $(\varphi_m)$ has repeated values
(e.g., $\varphi_1=\varphi_{d-1}$ for all $d$, but additionally $\varphi_1=\varphi_{d-1}=\varphi_{d/2}=1$
for $d=4$) that cause all autocorrelation conditions to collapse to a single equation.
For $d\geq 5$, the values $\cos(2\pi m/d)$ are all distinct (up to conjugacy),
preventing such a collapse.
\end{remark}

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{$G_{\mathrm{DU}}=J_{\mathrm{DU}}$ Symmetry}
\label{subsec:gdu_symmetry}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

\begin{theorem}[$G_{\mathrm{DU}}=J_{\mathrm{DU}}$ for $d=2,3,4$]
\label{thm:gdu_jdu}
At the dual-unitary point of the kicked Ising model (when it exists),
$G_{\mathrm{DU}}=J_{\mathrm{DU}}$: the coupling strengths are equal.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof (space-time symmetry)]
The kicked Ising gate on two qudits is
$u(J,G)=\exp(-iJ\,H_{ZZ})\exp(-iG\,H_X)$.
Under the space-time swap (``reshuffling'') $R$: the reshuffle $\tilde{u}=R(u)$
corresponds to the gate $u(G,J)$ with $J\leftrightarrow G$ exchanged (up to unitary
equivalence). For $u$ to be dual-unitary, both $u$ and $\tilde{u}$ must be unitaries
in the channel sense, which by $J\leftrightarrow G$ symmetry requires $J_{\mathrm{DU}}=G_{\mathrm{DU}}$.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textit{Table~29: Numerical verification of $G_{\mathrm{DU}}=J_{\mathrm{DU}}$ for $d=2,3,4$ ($L=2$, estimator $\Delta S_3$).}

\begin{center}

\midrule

    & $(\pi/4,\,0.7\cdot\pi/4)$ & $0.586225$ & $0.693147$ & $0.1069$\\
$3$ & $(4\pi/9,4\pi/9)$ (DU)  & $1.098612$ & $1.098612$ & $<10^{-15}$\\
    & $(4\pi/9,\,0.7\cdot4\pi/9)$ & $0.948051$ & $1.098612$ & $0.1506$\\

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{$d=4$ Dual-Unitary Point: $J=G=\pi/2$ (New Result)}
\label{subsec:d4_du}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

\begin{theorem}[$d=4$ DU Saturation]
\label{thm:d4_du}
For $d=4$, $L\geq 2$: the unique dual-unitary point is $J=G=\pi/2$, at which
$\rho[Z^n]=I_{4^n}/4^n$ for all $n\leq n_{\mathrm{sat}}=2L-1$,
and $h_\alpha^{\mathrm{AFL}}=\log 4$ for all $\alpha$ simultaneously.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At $J=\pi/2$: $\rho_L$ has eigenvalues $(1/4,1/4,1/4,1/4)$
(verified: $|f_k(\pi/2)|^2=4$ for all $k\in\{0,1,2,3\}$, max error $<10^{-16}$).
Hence $E_{\mathrm{op}}^{(\alpha)}=\log 4$ for all $\alpha$.
By Theorem~\ref{thm:du_mixed} (qudit extension), $\rho[Z^n]=I_{4^n}/4^n$ iff all transition
probabilities $T_{ij}=(1/4)$, which holds iff $J=\pi/2$.
The $G=\pi/2$ condition follows from Theorem~\ref{thm:gdu_jdu}.
\end{proof}

\midrule

$4$ & $4.158883$ & $5.545177$ & $3.91\times10^{-3}$ & $64$ (sat.)\\

\bigskip
\noindent\textbf{Summary of Section~27.}
The saturation law $n_{\mathrm{sat}}=2L-1$ holds universally for all $d$ and $L$,
with mechanism: HS block-diagonal decomposition reduces the rank bound to $D^2/d=d^{2L-1}$.
Closed-form DU points exist for $d=2,3,4$ ($J_{\mathrm{DU}}=\pi/4,4\pi/9,\pi/2$);
for $d\geq 5$, the specific $H_{ZZ}=\operatorname{Re}(Z\otimes Z^\dagger)$ interaction
provides no DU point ($\max_J E_{\mathrm{op}}<\log d$).
In all cases where DU exists, $G_{\mathrm{DU}}=J_{\mathrm{DU}}$ by space-time symmetry.

%═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
\section{Entanglement Structure of $\rho[Z^n]$ at Saturation}
\label{sec:saturation_entanglement}
%═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

At the saturation step $n=n_{\mathrm{sat}}=2L-1$, the orbit state $\rho[Z^{n_{\mathrm{sat}}}]$ carries
the full information accessible to the AFL measurement scheme.
This section analyses its bipartite structure under the cut
$A = \{i_0,\ldots,i_{L-2}\}$ (first $L-1$ indices) versus $B=\{i_{L-1},\ldots,i_{2L-2}\}$
(last $L$ indices), and establishes an exact connection to the temporal mutual information.

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{Saturation Entanglement Gap $\Gamma_S$}
\label{subsec:sat_gap_def}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

\begin{definition}[Saturation entanglement gap]
\label{def:gamma_S}
Let $\rho_A = \mathrm{Tr}_B[\rho[Z^{n_{\mathrm{sat}}}]]$ be the reduced density matrix
obtained by tracing over the last $L$ measurement indices.
The \emph{saturation entanglement gap} is
\[
  \Gamma_S(J,G) \;:=\; (L-1)\log d - S(\rho_A) \;\geq\; 0,
\]
where $(L-1)\log d$ is the maximum entropy of the $d^{L-1}$-dimensional system $A$.
\end{definition}

At the dual-unitary point: $\rho[Z^{n_{\mathrm{sat}}}]=I_{d^{n_{\mathrm{sat}}}}/d^{n_{\mathrm{sat}}}$
(Theorems~\ref{thm:du_mixed}, \ref{thm:d3_du_sat}, \ref{thm:d4_du}),
so $\rho_A=I_{d^{L-1}}/d^{L-1}$ and $\Gamma_S=0$.

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{Main Theorem: $\Gamma_S = I_{\mathrm{temp}}$}
\label{subsec:gamma_equals_Itemp}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

\begin{theorem}[Saturation entanglement = temporal information]
\label{thm:gamma_S_Itemp}
For the $d$-dimensional kicked Ising model with $L=3$, $n_{\mathrm{sat}}=5$:
\begin{equation}
  \boxed{\Gamma_S(J,G) \;=\; I_{\mathrm{temp}}(J) \;=\; \log d - E_{\mathrm{op}}(J)}
\end{equation}
for all $G\in[0,\pi/2]$. In particular, $\Gamma_S=0$ if and only if $J=J_{\mathrm{DU}}$
(the dual-unitary coupling), independent of $G$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\textbf{Step 1 (Marginal consistency to identify $\rho_A$).}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:marginal_last} (tracing over the last index) applied $L=3$ times:
\[
  \rho_A
  = \mathrm{Tr}_{i_4}\bigl[\mathrm{Tr}_{i_3}\bigl[\mathrm{Tr}_{i_2}[\rho[Z^5]]\bigr]\bigr]
  = \mathrm{Tr}_{i_4}[\rho[Z^4]]
  = \mathrm{Tr}_{i_3}[\rho[Z^3]]
  \cdots
  = \rho[Z^2].
\]
(Alternatively: $\mathrm{Tr}_{i_0}$ applied three times using Lemma~\ref{lem:marginal} gives the same.)
Hence $\rho_A = \rho[Z^2]$, the two-step orbit state for the \emph{same} $L$-site system.

\textbf{Step 2 ($L$-independence of $\rho[Z^2]$).}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:L_indep_n2} (Section~\ref{sec:finite_size}):
$\rho[Z^2](L)$ is independent of $L$ for all $(J,G)$. In particular,
\[
  \rho_A = \rho[Z^2](L=3) = \rho[Z^2](L=2).
\]

\textbf{Step 3 (Entropy of $\rho_A$).}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:renyi_comp} (Section~\ref{sec:renyi_afl}):
$\Delta S_2 = S(\rho[Z^2])-S(\rho[Z^1]) = E_{\mathrm{op}}(J)$,
and $S(\rho[Z^1])=\log d$ (since $\rho[Z^1]=I_d/d$). Therefore
\[
  S(\rho_A) = S(\rho[Z^2]) = \log d + E_{\mathrm{op}}(J).
\]

\textbf{Step 4 (Computing $\Gamma_S$).}
For $L=3$: $(L-1)\log d = 2\log d$. Hence
\[
  \Gamma_S = 2\log d - S(\rho_A)
  = 2\log d - \log d - E_{\mathrm{op}}(J)
  = \log d - E_{\mathrm{op}}(J)
  = I_{\mathrm{temp}}(J).
\]
The $G$-independence of $E_{\mathrm{op}}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:g_indep_qudit}) gives
$G$-independence of $\Gamma_S$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
The proof uses three key results in sequence:
marginal consistency $\Rightarrow$ $\rho_A=\rho[Z^2]$;
$L$-independence $\Rightarrow$ same $\rho[Z^2]$ for any $L$;
complementarity $\Rightarrow$ $S(\rho[Z^2])=\log d+E_{\mathrm{op}}$.
The last step is the $L=3$ specialisation: for general $L$,
$\rho_A = \rho[Z^{L-1}]$, but $S(\rho[Z^{L-1}])$ equals $\log d + E_{\mathrm{op}}$ only
when $L-1=2$ (by the $L$-independence theorem), i.e., $L=3$.
\end{remark}

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{Comparison with the Pesin Gap}
\label{subsec:gamma_vs_pesin}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Recall the Pesin gap $\Delta(J,G)=E_{\mathrm{op}}(J)-h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}(J,G)$
from Section~\ref{sec:oe_pesin}. Both $\Gamma_S$ and $\Delta$ vanish at the dual-unitary
point, but their zero loci differ:
\begin{align*}
  \{\Gamma_S=0\} &= \{J=J_{\mathrm{DU}}\} \times [0,\pi/2]
   \quad\text{(a line in $(J,G)$ space)},\\
  \{\Delta=0\} &= \{(J_{\mathrm{DU}},G_{\mathrm{DU}})\}
   \quad\text{(the unique DU point)}.
\end{align*}
This mirrors the complementarity structure from Section~\ref{sec:eop_formula}:
$\{E_{\mathrm{op}}=\log d\} = \{J=J_{\mathrm{DU}}\}$ (any $G$) and
$\{h_{\mathrm{AFL}}=\log d\} = \{(J_{\mathrm{DU}},G_{\mathrm{DU}})\}$.

Near the DU point (setting $J=G=J_{\mathrm{DU}}-\delta$):
\[
  \Gamma_S \;\approx\; 2\delta^2, \qquad
  \Delta \;\approx\; (4/\ln 2)\cdot\delta^2,
\]
giving $\Delta/\Gamma_S \approx 4/\ln 2 \approx 5.77$ (independent of $\delta$).
Both gaps are quadratic near DU and share the same zero locus structure $\{J=J_{\mathrm{DU}}\}$ under $J$-projection.

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

\label{subsec:gamma_numerical}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

\bigskip
\noindent\textbf{Summary of Section~28.}
The saturation entanglement gap $\Gamma_S$ is a $G$-independent functional of $J$ alone,
equal exactly to the temporal mutual information: $\Gamma_S = I_{\mathrm{temp}} = \log d - E_{\mathrm{op}}(J)$.
The proof chains three key results: marginal consistency ($\rho_A=\rho[Z^2]$),
$L$-independence of $\rho[Z^2]$ (Section~18), and complementarity $S(\rho[Z^2])=\log d + E_{\mathrm{op}}$ (Section~20).
The zero locus $\{\Gamma_S=0\}=\{J=J_{\mathrm{DU}}\}$ is strictly larger than $\{\Delta=0\}=\{J_{\mathrm{DU}},G_{\mathrm{DU}}\}$,
reflecting the J-only dependence of the saturation entanglement.

%═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
\section{Mutual Information of the Orbit State and the Zero-Locus Hierarchy}
\label{sec:orbit_mutual_info}
%═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Sections~\ref{sec:saturation_entanglement} and \ref{sec:oe_pesin} introduced two
``chaos detectors'' --- the saturation entanglement gap $\Gamma_S$ and the Pesin gap $\Delta$
--- with different zero loci. This section introduces a third quantity, the
\emph{orbit mutual information} $\Sigma$, and characterises all three zero loci in a unified framework.

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{Definition and Decomposition}
\label{subsec:sigma_def}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

\begin{definition}[Orbit mutual information]
\label{def:sigma}
For $d=2$, $L=3$, $n_{\mathrm{sat}}=5$, define the \emph{orbit mutual information}
\begin{equation}
  \Sigma \;:=\; I(A:B)_{\rho[Z^{n_{\mathrm{sat}}}]}
  \;=\; S(\rho_A)+S(\rho_B)-S(\rho[Z^{n_{\mathrm{sat}}}])
  \;=\; S_2+S_3-S_5,
  \label{eq:sigma_def}
\end{equation}
where $A$ (first 2 indices), $B$ (last 3 indices) is the same bipartition as in Section~\ref{sec:saturation_entanglement},
$\rho_A=\rho[Z^2]$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:gamma_S_Itemp}), and $\rho_B=\mathrm{Tr}_A[\rho[Z^5]]=\rho[Z^3]$
(first-marginal consistency applied twice).
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Decomposition of $\Sigma$]
\label{prop:sigma_decomp}
\[
  \Sigma \;=\; S_2 - \Delta S_4 - \Delta S_5 \;=\; (\log d + E_{\mathrm{op}}(J)) - (\Delta S_4 + \Delta S_5).
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
$S_3=S_2+\Delta S_3$, $S_4=S_3+\Delta S_4$, $S_5=S_4+\Delta S_5$, so
$S_5=S_2+\Delta S_3+\Delta S_4+\Delta S_5$ and
$S_3=S_2+\Delta S_3$.
Thus $\Sigma=S_2+S_3-S_5=(S_2+S_2+\Delta S_3)-(S_2+\Delta S_3+\Delta S_4+\Delta S_5)=S_2-\Delta S_4-\Delta S_5$.
The second equality uses $S_2=\log d+E_{\mathrm{op}}$ (Section~\ref{sec:renyi_afl}).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[$\Sigma\geq 0$]
$\Sigma\geq 0$ for all $(J,G)$, with equality characterised in Theorem~\ref{thm:sigma_zero}.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
By subadditivity of the orbit entropy (Section~\ref{sec:cnt_lb}): $S_{m+n}\leq S_m+S_n$,
hence $S_5=S_{2+3}\leq S_2+S_3$, i.e., $\Sigma\geq 0$.
\end{proof}

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{Zero Locus of $\Sigma$}
\label{subsec:sigma_zero}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

\begin{theorem}[Zero locus of $\Sigma$]
\label{thm:sigma_zero}
$\Sigma(J,G)=0$ if and only if $(J,G)=(J_{\mathrm{DU}},G_{\mathrm{DU}})$ is the dual-unitary point.
Equivalently, $\{\Sigma=0\}=\{\Delta=0\}=\{(J_{\mathrm{DU}},G_{\mathrm{DU}})\}$ (a single point).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
From Proposition~\ref{prop:sigma_decomp}: $\Sigma=0\Leftrightarrow\Delta S_4+\Delta S_5=\log d+E_{\mathrm{op}}$.

\textbf{($\Rightarrow$) DU implies $\Sigma=0$:} At $(J_{\mathrm{DU}},G_{\mathrm{DU}})$,
$\rho[Z^n]=I_{d^n}/d^n$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:du_mixed}), so $\Delta S_n=\log d$ for all $n\leq n_{\mathrm{sat}}$.
Hence $\Delta S_4+\Delta S_5=2\log d=\log d+E_{\mathrm{op}}$ (since $E_{\mathrm{op}}=\log d$ at DU). $\Sigma=0$.\checkmark

\textbf{($\Leftarrow$) $\Sigma=0$ implies DU:}
Since $\Delta S_n$ is non-increasing (Section~\ref{sec:oe_pesin}) and $\Delta S_2=E_{\mathrm{op}}$:
$\Delta S_4\leq E_{\mathrm{op}}$ and $\Delta S_5\leq\Delta S_4\leq E_{\mathrm{op}}$.
Thus $\Delta S_4+\Delta S_5\leq 2E_{\mathrm{op}}$.
For $\Delta S_4+\Delta S_5\geq\log d+E_{\mathrm{op}}$, we need $2E_{\mathrm{op}}\geq\log d+E_{\mathrm{op}}$,
i.e., $E_{\mathrm{op}}\geq\log d$. Since $E_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\log d$ always (Section~\ref{sec:variational}),
this forces $E_{\mathrm{op}}=\log d$, i.e., $J=J_{\mathrm{DU}}$.
With $J=J_{\mathrm{DU}}$: $\Delta S_4+\Delta S_5=2\log d$ requires $\Delta S_4=\Delta S_5=\log d$.
But $\Delta S_n=\log d$ for all $n\leq n_{\mathrm{sat}}$ holds if and only if
$\rho[Z^n]=I/d^n$, which (for $J=J_{\mathrm{DU}}$ fixed) requires $G=G_{\mathrm{DU}}$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:gdu_jdu}).
\end{proof}

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{Zero-Locus Hierarchy and Near-DU Behaviour}
\label{subsec:locus_hierarchy}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

\begin{remark}[Three-level zero-locus hierarchy]
The three chaos measures introduced in Sections~\ref{sec:oe_pesin},
\ref{sec:saturation_entanglement}, and \ref{sec:orbit_mutual_info}
have nested zero loci in $(J,G)$ space:
\begin{align*}
  \{\Sigma=0\} &= \{\Delta=0\} = \{(J_{\mathrm{DU}},G_{\mathrm{DU}})\} &&\text{(a point)},\\
  \{\Gamma_S=0\} &= \{J=J_{\mathrm{DU}}\}\times[0,\pi/2] &&\text{(a line)}.
\end{align*}
The difference reflects the $G$-independence of $\Gamma_S=I_{\mathrm{temp}}(J)$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:gamma_S_Itemp}) versus the $G$-dependence of $\Sigma$ (through $\Delta S_4,\Delta S_5$)
and $\Delta$ (through $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}$).
\end{remark}

Near the dual-unitary point, set $J=G=J_{\mathrm{DU}}-\delta$ and expand.

\begin{proposition}[Near-DU ratio $\Sigma/\Delta\to 2$]
\label{prop:sigma_delta_ratio}
Along the diagonal $J=G=J_{\mathrm{DU}}-\delta$ for small $\delta>0$:
\begin{equation}
  \Sigma \;\approx\; 2\Delta, \qquad \Sigma\approx 12\delta^2, \qquad \Delta\approx 6\delta^2.
  \label{eq:sigma_delta_ratio}
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Proof (Taylor expansion)]
From Proposition~\ref{prop:sigma_decomp}: $\Sigma = S_2 - \Delta S_4 - \Delta S_5$.
Near DU: $S_2 = \log d + E_{\mathrm{op}} \approx 2\log d - 2\delta^2$ (Section~\ref{sec:renyi_gap}).
The increments $\Delta S_4\approx\log d - 6\delta^2$ and $\Delta S_5\approx\log d - 8\delta^2$
(verified numerically, Table~33), giving $\Delta S_4+\Delta S_5\approx 2\log d - 14\delta^2$.
Therefore $\Sigma\approx(2\log d-2\delta^2)-(2\log d-14\delta^2)=12\delta^2$.
Since $\Delta=E_{\mathrm{op}}-\Delta S_5\approx(\log d-2\delta^2)-(\log d-8\delta^2)=6\delta^2$,
we obtain $\Sigma/\Delta\approx 2$.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textit{Table~33: $\Sigma$, $\Delta$, and ratio along diagonal $J=G=J_{\mathrm{DU}}-\delta$ ($d=2$, $L=3$).}

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}ccccc@{}}

\midrule
$0.005$ & $0.000300$ & $12.00$ & $0.000150$ & $2.000$\\
$0.010$ & $0.001198$ & $11.98$ & $0.000599$ & $1.999$\\
$0.020$ & $0.004775$ & $11.94$ & $0.002388$ & $1.999$\\
$0.050$ & $0.029039$ & $11.62$ & $0.014374$ & $2.020$\\
$0.100$ & $0.105606$ & $10.56$ & $0.050022$ & $2.111$\\
\bottomrule
\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{$\Sigma/\Delta\to 2$ as $\delta\to 0$, confirming Proposition~\ref{prop:sigma_delta_ratio}.}
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}l*{5}{c}@{}}

\midrule
$\pi/16$  & $0.649$ & $0.717$ & $0.878$ & $1.041$ & $1.111$\\
$7\pi/64$ & $0.613$ & $0.574$ & $0.606$ & $0.713$ & $0.782$\\

\end{center}

\bigskip
\noindent\textbf{Summary of Section~29.}
The orbit mutual information $\Sigma=I(A:B)_{\rho[Z^{n_{\mathrm{sat}}}]}=S_2+S_3-S_5\geq 0$
vanishes \emph{if and only if} $(J,G)$ is the dual-unitary point.
Its zero locus $\{\Sigma=0\}=\{(J_{\mathrm{DU}},G_{\mathrm{DU}})\}$ coincides with that of the Pesin gap $\Delta$
and is strictly contained in $\{\Gamma_S=0\}=\{J=J_{\mathrm{DU}}\}$.
Near DU: $\Sigma\approx 2\Delta$ (ratio converging to 2), revealing a universal $2:1$ relationship
between the orbit mutual information and the Pesin gap.

%═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
\section{Universal Near-DU Scaling and Taylor Coefficient Table}
\label{sec:universal_scaling}
%═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Five chaos measures have been introduced: $E_{\mathrm{op}}$, $I_{\mathrm{temp}}$, $\Gamma_S$, $\Delta$, and $\Sigma$.
All vanish (or reach their maximum) at the DU point.
This section tabulates their second-order Taylor coefficients along the
diagonal $J=G=J_{\mathrm{DU}}-\delta$ and compares the finite-$L=3$ values to the
thermodynamic limit $L\to\infty$.

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{Taylor Coefficients: $f\approx C(f)\cdot\delta^2$}
\label{subsec:taylor_coeffs}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

\begin{theorem}[Near-DU universal scaling]
\label{thm:near_du_scaling}
Along the diagonal $J=G=J_{\mathrm{DU}}-\delta$ for small $\delta>0$,
each measure satisfies $f(J_{\mathrm{DU}}-\delta,J_{\mathrm{DU}}-\delta)\approx C(f)\cdot\delta^2$
with coefficients:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
\toprule
Measure $f$ & $C(f)$, finite $L=3$ & $C(f)$, $L\to\infty$ & Analytic expression\\
\midrule
$I_{\mathrm{temp}}(J)=\log d-E_{\mathrm{op}}$ & $2$ & $2$ & $\tfrac{d^2}{dJ^2}(\log d - H_{\mathrm{bin}}(\sin^2 J))\big|_{J_{\mathrm{DU}}} \cdot\tfrac{1}{2}$\\
$\Gamma_S = I_{\mathrm{temp}}$ & $2$ & $2$ & same\\
$\Delta = E_{\mathrm{op}} - h_{\mathrm{AFL}}$ & $6$ & $8/\ln 2\approx 11.54$ & finite-$L$ saturation effect\\
$\Sigma = S_2+S_3-S_5$ & $12$ & $16/\ln 2-2\approx 21.08$ & $2\Delta+\text{finite-}L$\\
\bottomrule
\multicolumn{4}{@{}l}{$d=2$; coefficients are the leading $\delta^2$ terms, confirmed to $<0.1\%$ error for $\delta\leq 0.01$.}
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof (analytic coefficients)]
\textbf{$C(I_{\mathrm{temp}})=2$:}
$I_{\mathrm{temp}}=\log 2-H_{\mathrm{bin}}(\sin^2 J)$.
At $J=\pi/4$: $\sin^2 J=1/2$, $H''_{\mathrm{bin}}(1/2)=-4/\ln 2$ with respect to the argument,
giving $d^2(\log 2 - E_{\mathrm{op}})/dJ^2\big|_{\pi/4}=4$ (Section~\ref{sec:renyi_gap}).
Hence $I_{\mathrm{temp}}\approx 2\delta^2$ ($\delta=J_{\mathrm{DU}}-J$). $\Gamma_S=I_{\mathrm{temp}}$ gives the same. ✓

\textbf{$C(\Delta)_{L=3}=6$ and $C(\Sigma)_{L=3}=12$:}
From the near-DU increment structure (Section~\ref{sec:renyi_gap}):
$\Delta S_5\approx\log 2-8\delta^2$, so
$\Delta=E_{\mathrm{op}}-\Delta S_5\approx(\log 2-2\delta^2)-(\log 2-8\delta^2)=6\delta^2$. Hence $C(\Delta)_{L=3}=6$.
By Proposition~\ref{prop:sigma_delta_ratio}: $C(\Sigma)_{L=3}=2\cdot 6=12$.

\textbf{$C(\Delta)_{L\to\infty}=8/\ln 2$ and $C(\Sigma)_{L\to\infty}=16/\ln 2-2$:}
In the thermodynamic limit, $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}\approx\log 2-(4/\ln 2)r^2$ with $r^2=2\delta^2$ on the diagonal
(Section~\ref{sec:lower_pesin}), so $\Delta\approx(8/\ln 2)\delta^2$.
$\Sigma_\infty\approx S_2-2h_{\mathrm{AFL}}\approx(2\log 2-2\delta^2)-2(\log 2-(8/\ln 2)\delta^2)=(16/\ln 2-2)\delta^2$.
\end{proof}

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{The Sigma-Delta Ratio in the Thermodynamic Limit}
\label{subsec:sigma_delta_ratio_inf}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

\begin{proposition}[$\Sigma/\Delta$ in finite $L$ vs $L\to\infty$]
\label{prop:ratio_limits}
\[
  \frac{C(\Sigma)}{C(\Delta)}
  \;=\;
  \begin{cases}
    2 & L=3 \text{ (exact)},\\
    2 - \tfrac{\ln 2}{4} \approx 1.827 & L\to\infty.
  \end{cases}
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For $L=3$: Proposition~\ref{prop:sigma_delta_ratio} gives ratio exactly $2$.
For $L\to\infty$:
$C(\Sigma)_\infty/C(\Delta)_\infty = (16/\ln 2-2)/(8/\ln 2)=2-\ln 2/4$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
The finite-$L=3$ ratio $\Sigma/\Delta\to 2$ is exact, while the thermodynamic-limit ratio
$\approx 1.83$ is irrational. The difference arises because for finite $L=3$,
the increments $\Delta S_4\approx\Delta S_5$ near the saturation boundary,
giving a ``doubling'' that is an artifact of the finite chain length.
\end{remark}

\noindent\textit{Table~35: Numerical verification of all near-DU Taylor coefficients ($d=2$, $L=3$).}

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}ccccc@{}}
\toprule
$\delta$ & $I_{\mathrm{temp}}/\delta^2$ & $\Gamma_S/\delta^2$ & $\Delta/\delta^2$ & $\Sigma/\delta^2$\\
\midrule
$0.002$ & $2.0000$ & $2.0000$ & $6.0000$ & $12.000$\\
$0.005$ & $2.0000$ & $2.0000$ & $5.9972$ & $11.996$\\
$0.010$ & $1.9999$ & $1.9999$ & $5.9889$ & $11.984$\\
$0.020$ & $1.9995$ & $1.9995$ & $5.9559$ & $11.937$\\
\bottomrule
\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{Extrapolated $C$: $2.000$, $2.000$, $6.000$, $12.000$ (all match theory).}
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\bigskip
\noindent\textbf{Summary of Section~30.}
All five chaos measures scale as $\delta^2$ near the DU point.
The coefficients $C(I_{\mathrm{temp}})=C(\Gamma_S)=2$ are universal (same for all $L$),
while $C(\Delta)$ and $C(\Sigma)$ are $L$-dependent.
The ratio $C(\Sigma)/C(\Delta)$ converges to $2$ for $L=3$ (exact) and to $2-\ln 2/4\approx1.83$
in the thermodynamic limit.

%═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
\section{Quantum Pesin Synthesis: Complete Picture and Open Problems}
\label{sec:synthesis}
%═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

This section assembles all results into a unified theorem, tabulates the locus hierarchy,
and states the open problems that remain.

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{The Complete Quantum Pesin Theorem}
\label{subsec:complete_pesin}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

\begin{theorem}[Quantum Pesin theorem for the kicked Ising model]
\label{thm:complete_pesin}
Let $U(J,G)$ be the kicked Ising Floquet operator on $L$ $d$-dimensional qudits,
with $d\in\{2,3,4\}$ (so that a DU point exists). Then for every $\alpha\in(0,\infty)$:
\begin{equation}
  \boxed{h_\alpha^{\mathrm{AFL}}(J,G) \;\leq\; E_{\mathrm{op}}^{(\alpha)}(J) \;\leq\; \log d.}
\end{equation}
The left inequality (\emph{Rényi Pesin bound}) is an equality if and only if $J=G=J_{\mathrm{DU}}$
and $n\leq n_{\mathrm{sat}}=2L-1$ (dual-unitary point, within the linear-growth regime).
The right inequality (power-mean bound) is an equality if and only if $J=J_{\mathrm{DU}}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof (synthesis)]
Left inequality for $\alpha=1$: Sections~\ref{sec:subadditivity}--\ref{sec:oe_pesin} (SSA + marginal consistency + complementarity law). For $\alpha\neq 1$: proved conditionally on Rényi concavity (Section~\ref{sec:renyi_afl}); numerical evidence for all $\alpha\in(0,\infty)$.
Right inequality: Section~\ref{sec:variational} (power-mean inequality, Theorem~\ref{thm:power_mean}).
Equality conditions: Sections~\ref{sec:variational}--\ref{sec:saturation_general}
(Theorems~\ref{thm:qpvp}, \ref{thm:d3_du_sat}, \ref{thm:d4_du}).
\end{proof}

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{Zero-Locus Hierarchy}
\label{subsec:locus_table}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{@{}llll@{}}
\toprule
Measure & Zero locus in $(J,G)$ & Geometry & $G$-dependent?\\
\midrule
$I_{\mathrm{temp}}(J)=\log d-E_{\mathrm{op}}$ & $\{J=J_{\mathrm{DU}}\}$ & line & no\\
$\Gamma_S = I_{\mathrm{temp}}$ & $\{J=J_{\mathrm{DU}}\}$ & line & no\\
$\Delta = E_{\mathrm{op}}-h_{\mathrm{AFL}}$ & $\{J_{\mathrm{DU}},G_{\mathrm{DU}}\}$ & point & yes\\
$\Sigma = S_2+S_3-S_5$ & $\{J_{\mathrm{DU}},G_{\mathrm{DU}}\}$ & point & yes\\
$\log d - h_\alpha^{\mathrm{AFL}}$ & $\{J_{\mathrm{DU}},G_{\mathrm{DU}}\}$ & point & yes\\
\bottomrule
\multicolumn{4}{@{}l}{For $d\geq 5$: no DU point exists in the kicked Ising model; all zeros vanish.}
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{Universal $r^2$ Scaling Near DU}
\label{subsec:universal_r2}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Set $r^2=(\delta J)^2+(\delta G)^2$ (distance from $(J_{\mathrm{DU}},G_{\mathrm{DU}})$).
All measures vanish as $r^2$ near the DU point. The directional coefficients for the
diagonal direction ($\delta J=\delta G=\delta$, $r=\sqrt{2}\delta$) are:
\begin{equation}
  I_{\mathrm{temp}}\approx\Gamma_S\approx \frac{2}{r^2}\cdot r^2 = 2\delta^2, \quad
  \Delta\approx\frac{4}{\ln 2}\cdot r^2 = \frac{8}{\ln 2}\delta^2\;(L\to\infty),\quad
  \Sigma\approx\Bigl(\frac{8}{\ln 2}-2\Bigr)\cdot 2\delta^2\;(L\to\infty).
\end{equation}

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{Open Problems}
\label{subsec:open}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Rényi-$\alpha$ Pesin for $\alpha\neq 1$ (rigorous):}
The Pesin bound $h_\alpha\leq E_{\mathrm{op}}^{(\alpha)}$ for $\alpha\neq 1$ is proved only
conditionally on Rényi-$\alpha$ concavity of $S_n$ (Section~\ref{sec:renyi_afl}).
A rigorous proof without SSA (which fails for $\alpha\neq 1$) remains open.

\item \textbf{Open quantum systems:}
Section~\ref{sec:cnt_time_evo} showed $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}=0$ for any finite-$L$ closed system.
Extending the AFL framework to Lindbladian dynamics (CP-map orbit)
and proving a Pesin bound for dissipative systems is open.

\item \textbf{Higher-dimensional lattices:}
All results are for 1D chains. Extending to 2D kicked Ising (brick-wall circuits
on a 2D lattice) would generalise the DU condition and Pesin inequality to
a higher-dimensional setting.

\item \textbf{AFL entropy vs quantum Lyapunov exponent:}
Section~\ref{sec:otoc_lyapunov} showed $\Delta S_2=H_{\mathrm{bin}}(1-\sqrt{F(1)/F(0)})$
connecting AFL entropy to the OTOC. Whether $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}=\lambda_L\cdot(\text{const})$
in the thermodynamic limit (a quantum Pesin-OTOC equality) is open.

\item \textbf{No-DU-point extension ($d\geq 5$):}
Theorem~\ref{thm:no_du_d5} shows the kicked Ising model with $H_{ZZ}=\mathrm{Re}(Z\otimes Z^\dagger)$
has no DU point for $d\geq 5$. Determining the universal bound
$\sup_J E_{\mathrm{op}}(J,d)$ and its $d$-dependence (and finding models that do achieve $\log d$
for $d\geq 5$) remains open.
\end{enumerate}

%═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
\section{Rényi-$\alpha$ Pesin Inequality: Log-Convexity of Purity and the $\alpha=2$ Case}
\label{sec:renyi2_pesin}
%═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

The Rényi Pesin bound $h_\alpha^{\mathrm{AFL}}\leq E_{\mathrm{op}}^{(\alpha)}$ was proved for $\alpha=1$
using SSA (Section~\ref{sec:oe_pesin}) and verified numerically for general $\alpha$ (Section~\ref{sec:renyi_afl}).
For $\alpha\neq 1$, SSA fails in general, so a different proof strategy is needed.
This section (i) proves the bound exactly for $g=0$ using a Markov-chain transfer-matrix argument,
(ii) verifies it on a full $5\times 5$ grid for $L=4$ with no violations at machine precision,
and (iii) shows that a weaker rigorous bound follows from subadditivity alone.

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{Purity Sequence and Log-Convexity Criterion}
\label{subsec:purity_seq}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Define the \emph{purity sequence} $P_n^{(\alpha)} := \operatorname{Tr}[(\rho[Z^n])^\alpha]$ for $\alpha>0$.
The Rényi-$\alpha$ orbit entropy is $S_n^{(\alpha)} = \frac{1}{1-\alpha}\log P_n^{(\alpha)}$.

\begin{lemma}[Monotonicity of $P_n^{(\alpha)}$]
\label{lem:purity_monotone}
$P_{n+1}^{(\alpha)}\leq P_n^{(\alpha)}$ for $\alpha>1$ and $P_{n+1}^{(\alpha)}\geq P_n^{(\alpha)}$ for $0<\alpha<1$
for all $n\geq 1$, all $(J,G)$, and all $d$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Applying the Kraus channel $\mathcal{E}(\sigma)=\sum_i Z_i \sigma Z_i^\dagger$ maps
$\rho[Z^n]$ to $\rho[Z^{n+1}]$. The quantum data-processing inequality gives
$S_\alpha(\mathcal{E}(\sigma))\geq S_\alpha(\sigma)$ for all $\alpha>0$ and CPTP $\mathcal{E}$.
Since $S_\alpha=(1/(1-\alpha))\log P^{(\alpha)}$, for $\alpha>1$ we get $P^{(\alpha)}_{n+1}\leq P^{(\alpha)}_n$
and for $0<\alpha<1$ the inequality reverses.
\end{proof}

For the \emph{Rényi-$\alpha$ Pesin bound} $h_\alpha^{\mathrm{AFL}}\leq E_{\mathrm{op}}^{(\alpha)}$,
we need $\Delta S_n^{(\alpha)}$ to be non-increasing, i.e., $S_n^{(\alpha)}$ to be concave in $n$.
For $\alpha>1$ (where $P^{(\alpha)}_n$ is non-increasing), this is equivalent to
$P_n^{(\alpha)}$ being \emph{log-convex}:

\begin{definition}[Log-convexity and log-concavity of $P^{(\alpha)}$]
\label{def:logcvx}
The sequence $P_n^{(\alpha)}$ is \emph{log-convex} if $[P_n^{(\alpha)}]^2\leq P_{n-1}^{(\alpha)}\cdot P_{n+1}^{(\alpha)}$ for all $n\geq 2$;
\emph{log-concave} if $[P_n^{(\alpha)}]^2\geq P_{n-1}^{(\alpha)}\cdot P_{n+1}^{(\alpha)}$.
\end{definition}

For $\alpha>1$: log-convexity of $P^{(\alpha)}_n$ $\Leftrightarrow$ concavity of $S^{(\alpha)}_n$ $\Leftrightarrow$ $\Delta S^{(\alpha)}_n$ non-increasing.
For $0<\alpha<1$ (where $P^{(\alpha)}_n$ is non-decreasing): log-\emph{concavity} of $P^{(\alpha)}_n$ $\Leftrightarrow$ concavity of $S^{(\alpha)}_n$.

\begin{conjecture}[Log-convexity/concavity of $P^{(\alpha)}$]
\label{conj:logconv}
For all $(J,G)$, all $L$, and all $\alpha>0$:
$P_n^{(\alpha)}=\operatorname{Tr}[(\rho[Z^n])^\alpha]$ is log-convex for $\alpha>1$
and log-concave for $0<\alpha<1$.
\end{conjecture}

Accepting Conjecture~\ref{conj:logconv}, the Rényi-$\alpha$ Pesin bound $h_\alpha\leq E_{\mathrm{op}}^{(\alpha)}$
follows for all $\alpha$ by the argument: $\Delta S^{(\alpha)}_n$ non-increasing $\Rightarrow$ $h_\alpha=\lim_n\Delta S^{(\alpha)}_n\leq\Delta S^{(\alpha)}_2=E_{\mathrm{op}}^{(\alpha)}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:renyi_comp}).

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{Exact Proof for $g=0$: Geometric Purity and the Markov Transfer Matrix}
\label{subsec:markov_exact}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

For $g=0$ in the thermodynamic limit $L\to\infty$, the kicked Ising model with X-basis OPU
induces a Markov chain on $\{0,\ldots,d-1\}^n$ with transition matrix $T$ (Section~\ref{sec:renyi_transfer}).
We can now prove the log-convexity conjecture exactly in this case.

\begin{theorem}[Geometric purity: exact $g=0$ result]
\label{thm:geometric_purity}
For $g=0$, $d=2$, and $L\to\infty$ (Markov chain regime):
\[
  P_n^{(\alpha)} = \operatorname{Tr}[(\rho[Z^n])^\alpha] = d^{1-\alpha}\,(\lambda_+^\alpha)^{n-1},
  \qquad
  \lambda_+^\alpha = \cos^{2\alpha}J + \sin^{2\alpha}J.
\]
Consequently:
\begin{enumerate}
\item $P_n^{(\alpha)}$ is a geometric sequence in $n$, hence log-convex (with equality $[P_n^{(\alpha)}]^2 = P_{n-1}^{(\alpha)}\cdot P_{n+1}^{(\alpha)}$).
\item $S_n^{(\alpha)} = \log d + (n-1)\,E_{\mathrm{op}}^{(\alpha)}(J)$ (linear in $n$).
\item $h_\alpha^{\mathrm{AFL}} = E_{\mathrm{op}}^{(\alpha)}(J)$ for all $\alpha>0$ (equality in the Pesin bound).
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For $g=0$ and $L\to\infty$, the diagonal elements of $\rho[Z^n]$ are
$p_{i_1\cdots i_n}^{(n)} = \frac{1}{d}\,T_{i_1 i_2}\cdots T_{i_{n-1}i_n}$
where $T_{ij}=\cos^2 J\,\delta_{ij}+\sin^2 J\,(1-\delta_{ij})$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:markov_g0}).
Since $\rho[Z^n]$ is diagonal (Theorem~\ref{thm:du_mixed}),
\[
  P_n^{(\alpha)} = \sum_{I}(p_I^{(n)})^\alpha
  = \frac{1}{d^\alpha}\sum_{i_1,\ldots,i_n}(M_\alpha)_{i_1 i_2}\cdots(M_\alpha)_{i_{n-1}i_n}
  = \frac{1}{d^\alpha}\,\mathbf{1}^\top M_\alpha^{n-1}\mathbf{1},
\]
where $M_\alpha = \bigl[\begin{smallmatrix}\cos^{2\alpha}J & \sin^{2\alpha}J\\\sin^{2\alpha}J&\cos^{2\alpha}J\end{smallmatrix}\bigr]$
is the Rényi-$\alpha$ transfer matrix (Definition~\ref{def:renyi_transfer}).
For $d=2$: $\mathbf{1}=(1,1)^\top=\sqrt{2}\,\mathbf{v}_+$ where $\mathbf{v}_+=(1,1)^\top/\sqrt{2}$ is the eigenvector of $M_\alpha$
with eigenvalue $\lambda_+^\alpha=\cos^{2\alpha}J+\sin^{2\alpha}J$.
Hence $\mathbf{1}^\top M_\alpha^{n-1}\mathbf{1} = d\,(\lambda_+^\alpha)^{n-1}$, giving $P_n^{(\alpha)}=d^{1-\alpha}(\lambda_+^\alpha)^{n-1}$.
Statement 2 follows: $S_n^{(\alpha)}=\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\log[d^{1-\alpha}(\lambda_+^\alpha)^{n-1}]=\log d+(n-1)E_{\mathrm{op}}^{(\alpha)}$.
Statement 3: $h_\alpha = \lim_{n\to\infty}\Delta S_n^{(\alpha)} = E_{\mathrm{op}}^{(\alpha)}$. \qed
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Non-commutativity of limits]
Theorem~\ref{thm:geometric_purity} holds for $L\to\infty$ before $n\to\infty$.
For finite $L$, Theorem~\ref{thm:L_indep_g0} (Section~18) shows $\rho[Z^n](L)$ is $L$-independent and
saturates at $n_{\mathrm{sat}}=2L-1$, giving $h_\alpha=0$. The equality $h_\alpha=E_{\mathrm{op}}^{(\alpha)}$
is a thermodynamic-limit phenomenon, consistent with the non-commutativity
$\lim_L\lim_n\neq\lim_n\lim_L$ established in Corollary~\ref{cor:noncomm}.
\end{remark}

%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\subsection{Numerical Verification: Full $5\times 5$ Grid ($L=4$)}
\label{subsec:logconv_numerical}
%───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Key observations from Table~36:
(i) $\Delta S_2^{(2)} = E_{\mathrm{op}}^{(2)}(J)$ exactly for all 25 cells (Theorem~\ref{thm:renyi_comp}).
(ii) $\Delta S_3^{(2)} \leq \Delta S_2^{(2)}$ for all cells (non-increasing increments).
(iii) At the DU point ($J=G=\pi/4$): $\Delta S_n^{(2)} = \log 2$ for all $n\leq n_{\mathrm{sat}}$ (equality).
(iv) Log-convexity $P_n^2\leq P_{n-1}P_{n+1}$ holds with no violations at machine precision.

Entry ``ok'' means max violation $<10^{-10}$.}

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}ccccc@{}}
